<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

Divide $21060$ by $9$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{21060}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2340 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2340 \times \cancel 9}{\cancel 9} \\\\
&= 2340
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2340$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{21060}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2340 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2340 \times \cancel 9}{\cancel 9} \\\\
&= 2340
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

Circle two numbers which have the same value.

$
8 \dfrac{1}{20} \quad 
\dfrac{17}{2}   \quad 
8.1             \quad 
8.05            \quad
8 \dfrac{1}{5}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's convert the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 8 \dfrac {1} {20} \quad \dfrac {17} {2} \quad 8.1 \quad 8.05 \quad \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
&= 8.05 \quad 8.5 \quad 8.1 \quad 8.05 \quad 0.2
\end{aligned}
$

Equal numbers $= 8 \dfrac {1} {20}, \quad 8.05$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \dfrac {1} {20}, \quad 8.05$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's convert the numbers into decimals.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 8 \dfrac {1} {20} \quad \dfrac {17} {2} \quad 8.1 \quad 8.05 \quad \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
&= 8.05 \quad 8.5 \quad 8.1 \quad 8.05 \quad 0.2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Equal numbers $= 8 \dfrac {1} {20}, \quad 8.05$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \dfrac {1} {20}, \quad 8.05$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Put the correct number in the box.

$\boxed{\phantom{55}} + 18 = 8 \times 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 18 &= 8 \times 4 \\\\
a + 18 &= 32 \\\\
a      &= 32 - 18 \\\\
a      &= 14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

What is $\dfrac{3}{7}$  of $385?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {7} \ \text {of} \ 385 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {7} \times 385 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 385} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 55 \times 7} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 55 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 55 \\\\
&= 165
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$165$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {7} \ \text {of} \ 385 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {7} \times 385 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 385} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 55 \times 7} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 55 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 55 \\\\
&= 165
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

In a survey, the hair colour of a set of girls was recorded. 
The results are shown on the bar chart below.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-13-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-13-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-13-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table below.

| **Hair Colour** | **Number of Girls** |
|:---------------:|:-------------------:|
|      Black      |         $11$        |
|    Dark brown   |         $15$        |
|   Light brown   |         $9$         |
|      Blonde     |         $10$        |
|       Red       |                     |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|   Hair Colour   |   Number of Girls   |
|:---------------:|:-------------------:|
|      Black      |         $11$        |
|    Dark brown   |         $15$        |
|   Light brown   |         $9$         |
|      Blonde     |         $10$        |
|       Red       |         $5$         |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Red $=$ $5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>|   Hair Colour   |   Number of Girls   |
|:---------------:|:-------------------:|
|      Black      |         $11$        |
|    Dark brown   |         $15$        |
|   Light brown   |         $9$         |
|      Blonde     |         $10$        |
|       Red       |         $5$         |
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Red</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of girls have brown hair?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% of girl with brown hair}               &= \dfrac {9 + 15} {11 + 15 + 9 + 10 + 5} \times 100 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {50} \times 100 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {50} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                                &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 48 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% of girl with brown hair}               &= \dfrac {9 + 15} {11 + 15 + 9 + 10 + 5} \times 100 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {50} \times 100 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {50} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                                &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 48 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

There are $200$ pupils on a school trip. Each coach only holds $34$ pupils. 
How many coaches will the school need to book?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Coaches required} &= \dfrac {200} {34} \\\\
                                   &= 5 \text{ remainder } 30 \\\\
                                   &\approx 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Coaches required} &= \dfrac {200} {34} \\\\
                                   &= 5 \text{ remainder } 30 \\\\
                                   &\approx 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

What fraction of the shape is shaded?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-15-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-15-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-15-00.png") 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total boxes}      &= 3 \times 4 + 1 + 1 + 4 \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                        &= 12 + 2 + 2 \\\\
                        &= 16 \\\\
\text{Shaded boxes}     &= 3 \times 2 + 1 + 1 + 8 \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                        &= 6 + 2 + 4 \\\\
                        &= 12 \\\\
\text{Fraction shaded}  &= \dfrac{12}{16} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {4 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {4}} {4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {3} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total boxes}      &= 3 \times 4 + 1 + 1 + 4 \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                        &= 12 + 2 + 2 \\\\
                        &= 16 \\\\
\text{Shaded boxes}     &= 3 \times 2 + 1 + 1 + 8 \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                        &= 6 + 2 + 4 \\\\
                        &= 12 \\\\
\text{Fraction shaded}  &= \dfrac{12}{16} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {4 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {4}} {4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {3} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The difference between two numbers is $7$.

When the numbers are multiplied, the result is $60$. 

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b &= 60 \\\\
a - b      &= 7  
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $60$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $7$.

Factors of $60 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60$

Factor pairs of $60$ that has difference of $7 = (3,10) \text{ and } (5, 12)$ 

| Factors   |  Difference  |     Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------:  |
| $3, 10$   |    $7$       |       30          |
| $5, 12$   |    $7$       |       60          |

A quick check shows that $5$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 5,12$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5, 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the second number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b &= 60 \\\\
a - b      &= 7  
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $60$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $7$.
<emptyline>
Factors of $60 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $60$ that has difference of $7 = (3,10) \text{ and } (5, 12)$ 
<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Difference  |     Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------:  |
| $3, 10$   |    $7$       |       30          |
| $5, 12$   |    $7$       |       60          |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $5$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two numbers are $= 5,12$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Joanna gets $\pounds 5.00$ pocket money each week and 
her younger sister, Amy, gets $\pounds 3.50$ each week. 
After $5 \ \text{weeks}$, Joanna saved all her money, but Amy 
spent $75 \ \text{p}$ a week on stickers. 

How much more does Joanna now have compared to Amy? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Difference}  &= 5 \times \pounds 5 - (5 \times \pounds 3.50 - 5 \times 75 \ \text{p}) \\\\
                    &= \pounds 25 - (\pounds 17.50 - 5 \times \pounds 0.75) \\\\
                    &= 25 - (17.50 - 3.75) \\\\
                    &= 25 - 13.75 \\\\
                    &= \pounds 11.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 11.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Difference}  &= 5 \times \pounds 5 - (5 \times \pounds 3.50 - 5 \times 75 \ \text{p}) \\\\
                    &= \pounds 25 - (\pounds 17.50 - 5 \times \pounds 0.75) \\\\
                    &= 25 - (17.50 - 3.75) \\\\
                    &= 25 - 13.75 \\\\
                    &= \pounds 11.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 11.25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

The next term is calculated by adding $5$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$

&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+5)} }  
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+5)} }

\end{matrix}

\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 23 + 5 \\\\
                                  &= 28 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}      &= 28 + 5 \\\\
                                  &= 33
\end{aligned}

&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(\times 4)} }

\end{matrix}

\begin{aligned}

                                  &= \dfrac {4} {4} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                                  &= 1 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}      &= 1 \times 4 \\\\
                                  &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Let the input number $= a$

Let the output number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 5) \times 2 &= 10 \\\\
a + 5            &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
a + 5            &= \dfrac {5 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a + 5            &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a + 5            &= 5 \\\\
a                &= 5 - 5 \\\\
a                &= 0
\end{aligned}
$


$
\begin{aligned}
b &= (10 - 8) \div 2 \\\\
b &= 2 \div 2 \\\\
b &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

Input number $=0$

Onput number $=1$

<div class='answer'>

$0, \ 10, \ 2, \ 1$

<div class='question question'>

Belinda has a digital alarm clock. 

The numbers on the clock are displayed as:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-22-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-22-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-22-00.png")

Belinda's digital clock shows the time $01{:}10$

She puts it upside down on her bedside table.

The same time is shown.

At what times between $01{:}10$ and $12{:}00$ does the clock 
show the same time when it is upside down as when it is the 
right way up?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

This shape has an area of $63 \ \text{cm}^2$.

It is made from square tiles.

Find the perimeter of the shape.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-26-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-26-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-26-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


Let the side of square tile $= s$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
7 \times (s \times s)    &= 63 \\\\
7 \times s^2             &= 63 \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= \dfrac {63} {7} \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= \dfrac {7 \times 9} {7} \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {7} \times 9} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= 9 \\\\
s                        &= \sqrt 9 \\\\
s                        &= 3 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

*TODO: add a description to count all the sides which add upto perimeter*

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of the shape} &= 16 \times s \\\\
                              &= 16 \times 3 \\\\
                              &= 48 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded
<emptyline>
Let the side of square tile $= s$
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
7 \times (s \times s)    &= 63 \\\\
7 \times s^2             &= 63 \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= \dfrac {63} {7} \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= \dfrac {7 \times 9} {7} \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {7} \times 9} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
s ^ 2                    &= 9 \\\\
s                        &= \sqrt 9 \\\\
s                        &= 3 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
*TODO: add a description to count all the sides which add upto perimeter*
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of the shape} &= 16 \times s \\\\
                              &= 16 \times 3 \\\\
                              &= 48 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-27-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-27-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-27-00.png") 

$48$ cats eat $12$ cans of food in $3 \ \text{days}$. 

Fill in the spaces:
  
$12$ cats eat 
$\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$
cans of food in $3 \ \text{days}$.

$48$ cats eat $24$ cans of food in 
$\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore} \ \text{days}$.

$24$ cats eat 
$\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$
cans of food in $18 \ \text{days}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of cats}                   &:& \text{cans of food} \\\\
= & 48                                      &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {12} {48} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 12} {48} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 4 \times 3} {12 \times 4} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 3
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cans of food}                     &:& \text{number of days} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 3 \\\\
= & 12 \times 2                             &:& 3 \times 2 \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& 6
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of cats}                     &:& \text{cans of food}                       &:& \text{number of days} \\\\
= & 48                                        &:& 12                                        &:& 3 \\\\
= & \dfrac {48} {2}                           &:& \dfrac {12} {2}                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 24} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2}                   &:& 3 \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 24} {\cancel 2}  &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2}   &:& 3 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 6                                         &:& 3 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 6 \times 6                                &:& 3 \times 6 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 36                                        &:& 18 \\\\
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3, 6, 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of cats}                   &:& \text{cans of food} \\\\
= & 48                                      &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {12} {48} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 12} {48} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 4 \times 3} {12 \times 4} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 3
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cans of food}                     &:& \text{number of days} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 3 \\\\
= & 12 \times 2                             &:& 3 \times 2 \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& 6
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of cats}                     &:& \text{cans of food}                       &:& \text{number of days} \\\\
= & 48                                        &:& 12                                        &:& 3 \\\\
= & \dfrac {48} {2}                           &:& \dfrac {12} {2}                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 24} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2}                   &:& 3 \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 24} {\cancel 2}  &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2}   &:& 3 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 6                                         &:& 3 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 6 \times 6                                &:& 3 \times 6 \\\\
= & 24                                        &:& 36                                        &:& 18 \\\\
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3, 6, 36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Two identical square holes are cut from a rectangular sheet leaving an 
area of $58 \ \text{square centimetres}$. How long are the sides of the square?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-29-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-29-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-29-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


Let the side of the square hole $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of two square holes} &= \text{Area of the rectangular sheet} - \text{Left area} \\\\
2 \times a ^ 2                  &= 10 \times 9 - 58 \\\\
2 \times a ^ 2                  &= 90 - 58 \\\\
2 \times a ^ 2                  &= 32 \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {32} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                               &= \sqrt 16 \\\\
a                               &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>

$

2 \times a ^ 2                  &= 32 \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {32} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                               &= \sqrt 16 \\\\
a                               &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

after $1 \ \text{cm}$, then bends the wire

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-31-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-31-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-31-00.png")

|Number of Bends         |$1$|$2$|$3$|$4$ |
|:----------------------:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:--:|
|Centimetres of wire used|   |   |   |$15$|


How much wire will she have used after $6$ bends?
 
How many bends will Jane have made if she uses $66 \ \text{cm}$ of wire?

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2:1:32</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Some fairy cakes are delivered for Janey's party.

Janey can use big plates or small plates to put the cakes on.

If she uses big plates she can put seven cakes on each plate with five left over.

If she uses small plates she can put five cakes on each plate with three left over.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png")

What is the smallest number of cakes that were delivered for Janey's party?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of big plates $= a$

Let the number of small plates $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5b + 3         &= 7a + 5 \\\\
5b             &= 7a + 5 - 3 \\\\
5b             &= 7a + 2 \\\\
5b - 7a        &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the value using trial and error. Lets find out the value of a and b that satisfies the above equation.

| $a$  | $b$ |                   $5b - 7a$                  |
|:----:|:---:|:--------------------------------------------:|
| $0$  | $1$ |    $5 \times 1 - 7 \times 0 = 5 - 0 = 5$     |
| $1$  | $2$ |    $5 \times 2 - 7 \times 1 = 10 - 7 = 3$    |
| $2$  | $3$ |    $5 \times 3 - 7 \times 2 = 15 - 14 = 1$   |
| $2$  | $4$ |    $5 \times 4 - 7 \times 2 = 20 - 14 = 6$   |
| $3$  | $5$ |    $5 \times 5 - 7 \times 3 = 25 - 21 = 4$   |
| $4$  | $6$ |    $5 \times 6 - 7 \times 4 = 30 - 28 = 2$   |

A quick check shows that $a = 4$ and $b = 6$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest number of cakes delivered}         &= 7 \times a + 5 \\\\
                                                  &= 7 \times 4 + 5 \\\\
                                                  &= 28 + 5 \\\\
                                                  &= 33
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$33$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of big plates $= a$
<emptyline>

$

| $1$  | $2$ |    $5 \times 2 - 7 \times 1 = 10 - 7 = 3$    |
| $2$  | $3$ |    $5 \times 3 - 7 \times 2 = 15 - 14 = 1$   |
| $2$  | $4$ |    $5 \times 4 - 7 \times 2 = 20 - 14 = 6$   |
| $3$  | $5$ |    $5 \times 5 - 7 \times 3 = 25 - 21 = 4$   |

                                                  &= 7 \times 4 + 5 \\\\

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The sweet shapes below cost $52 \ \text{p}$ altogether. Each heart 
costs half the price of a triangle and each star costs double the 
cost of each triangle. 

What is the cost of a heart?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-33-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-33-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-33-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of each triangle $= t$

Cost of each heart $= \dfrac {t} {2}$

Cost of each star $= 2t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times t + 4 \times \dfrac {t} {2} + 4 \times 2t           &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + \dfrac {4 t} {2} + 8 t                                &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + \dfrac {2t \times 2} {2} + 8 t                        &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + \dfrac {2t \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} + 8 t        &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + 2 t + 8 t                                             &= 52 \\\\
13 t                                                         &= 52 \\\\                                                           
t                                                            &= \dfrac{52}{13} \\\\
t                                                            &= \dfrac{13 \times 4}{13} \\\\ 
t                                                            &= \dfrac{\cancel {13} \times 4}{\cancel {13}} \\\\ 
t                                                            &= 4p                                                                                 
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of each heart} &= \dfrac {t} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                          &= 2 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of each triangle $= t$
<emptyline>
Cost of each heart $= \dfrac {t} {2}$
<emptyline>
Cost of each star $= 2t$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times t + 4 \times \dfrac {t} {2} + 4 \times 2t           &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + \dfrac {4 t} {2} + 8 t                                &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + \dfrac {2t \times 2} {2} + 8 t                        &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + \dfrac {2t \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} + 8 t        &= 52 \\\\
3 t  + 2 t + 8 t                                             &= 52 \\\\
13 t                                                         &= 52 \\\\                                                           
t                                                            &= \dfrac{52}{13} \\\\
t                                                            &= \dfrac{13 \times 4}{13} \\\\ 
t                                                            &= \dfrac{\cancel {13} \times 4}{\cancel {13}} \\\\ 
t                                                            &= 4p                                                                                 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of each heart} &= \dfrac {t} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                          &= 2 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2 \ \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A factory recycles paper cups for use in its canteen. Seven used cups 
are needed to make each new cup. From $721$ used cups, how many new cups 
can be made in total, if all the used cups get recycled?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total new cups} &= \dfrac {721} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                      &= 103
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$103$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total new cups} &= \dfrac {721} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                      &= 103
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$103$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

I bought a packet of fruit gums and ate $2$ of them, secretly, in my Music lesson.

At break, I shared one third of the remaining fruit gums with 
my friends. I then finished the fruit gums, eating an equal amount in each of 
the next four lessons.

What is the smallest number of fruit gums there could have been in the packet?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of gums $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((a - 2) \times (1 - \dfrac{1}{3})) \div 4 \\\\
&= ((a - 2) \times (\dfrac{1 \times 3}{1\times 3} - \dfrac{1}{3}))  \div 4 \\\\
&= ((a - 2) \times (\dfrac{3 - 1}{3})) \div 4 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2(a - 2)}{3} \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2(a - 2)}{3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2(a - 2)}{3 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times (a - 2)}{3 \times \cancel 2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{a-2}{6}
\end{aligned}
$

For smallest number we will make the above equation equal to $1$.

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 2) \times \dfrac{1}{6}   &= 1 \\\\
(a - 2)                       &= 1 \times 6 \\\\
a                             &= 6 + 2 \\\\
a                             &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of gums $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

&= ((a - 2) \times (\dfrac{3 - 1}{3})) \div 4 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2(a - 2)}{3} \times \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2(a - 2)}{3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2(a - 2)}{3 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times (a - 2)}{3 \times \cancel 2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{a-2}{6}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
For smallest number we will make the above equation equal to $1$.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 2) \times \dfrac{1}{6}   &= 1 \\\\
(a - 2)                       &= 1 \times 6 \\\\
a                             &= 6 + 2 \\\\
a                             &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Pia has three cards. Card $A$ tells her to 
multiply by $5$, card $B$ tells her to add $3$ and Card $C$ tells her to divide by $2$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-38-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-38-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2/section-1-question-38-00.png")

Pia arranges her cards in the order $A \ B \ C$ and starts with the number $3$. Her answer is $9$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Pia keeps her cards in the order $A \ B \ C$ and starts with $11$, what is her answer?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((11 \times 5) + 3) \div 2 \\\\
&= (55 + 3) \div 2 \\\\
&= 58 \div 2 \\\\
&= \dfrac{58}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{29 \times 2}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{29 \times  \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
&= 29
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((11 \times 5) + 3) \div 2 \\\\
&= (55 + 3) \div 2 \\\\
&= 58 \div 2 \\\\
&= \dfrac{58}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{29 \times 2}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{29 \times  \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
&= 29
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$29$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What whole number would she need to start with to get an answer of $19$, if 
her cards were still arranged in the order $A \ B \ C$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the starting number $= a$

As per the question, 

$
\begin{aligned}
((a \times 5) + 3) \div 2 &= 19 \\\\
(a \times 5) + 3          &= 19 \times 2 \\\\
(a \times 5) + 3          &= 38 \\\\
a \times 5                &= 38 - 3 \\\\
a \times 5                &= 35 \\\\
a                         &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
a                         &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                         &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                         &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the starting number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
((a \times 5) + 3) \div 2 &= 19 \\\\
(a \times 5) + 3          &= 19 \times 2 \\\\
(a \times 5) + 3          &= 38 \\\\
a \times 5                &= 38 - 3 \\\\
a \times 5                &= 35 \\\\
a                         &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
a                         &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                         &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                         &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Pia arranges her cards in the order $C \ B \ A$ and starts with number $4$, what is her answer?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((4 \div 2) + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{4}{2} + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{2 \times 2}{2} + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{2 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (2 + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= 5 \times 5 \\\\
&= 25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((4 \div 2) + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{4}{2} + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{2 \times 2}{2} + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (\dfrac{2 \times \cancel 2}{\cancel 2} + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= (2 + 3) \times 5 \\\\
&= 5 \times 5 \\\\
&= 25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Pia changes the order of the cards. She starts with a whole number and 
ends up with $15$. Give all the possible orders in which she could have used her three cards.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Possible arrangement are $= \text{BAC, BCA,  CBA}$

Let's verify,

Let the whole number $= a$

$\text{BAC} \rightarrow $

$
\begin{aligned}
((a + 3) \times 5) \div 2      &= 15 \\\\
(a + 3) \times \dfrac{5}{2}    &= 15 \\\\
(a + 3) \times 5               &= 15 \times 2 \\\\
(a + 3) \times 5               &= 30 \\\\
5a + 15                        &= 30 \\\\
5a                             &= 30 - 15 \\\\
5a                             &= 15 \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac{15}{5} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac{5 \times 3}{5}\\\\
a                              &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 3}{\cancel 5}\\\\
a                              &= 5 
\end{aligned}
$

$\text{BCA} \rightarrow $

$
\begin{aligned}
(((a + 3) \div 2 ) \times 5 )  &= 15 \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= \dfrac{15}{5} \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= \dfrac{5 \times 3}{5} \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 3}{\cancel 5} \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= 3 \\\\
(a + 3)                        &= 3 \times 2 \\\\
a + 3                          &= 6 \\\\
a                              &= 6 - 3 \\\\
a                              &= 3                    
\end{aligned}
$

$\text{CBA} \rightarrow $ 

$
\begin{aligned}
(((a \div 2 ) + 3) \times 5 )   &= 15 \\\\
(a \div 2 ) + 3                 &= \dfrac{15}{5} \\\\
(a \div 2 ) + 3                 &= \dfrac{5 \times 3}{5} \\\\
(a \div 2 ) + 3                 &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 3}{\cancel 5} \\\\
(a \div 2 ) + 3                 &= 3 \\\\
a \div 2                        &= 3 - 3 \\\\
a \div 2                        &= 0 \\\\
a                               &= 0 \times 2 \\\\
a                               &= 0
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{BAC, BCA,  CBA}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Possible arrangement are $= \text{BAC, BCA,  CBA}$
<emptyline>
Let's verify,
<emptyline>
Let the whole number $= a$
<emptyline>
$\text{BAC} \rightarrow $
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
((a + 3) \times 5) \div 2      &= 15 \\\\
(a + 3) \times \dfrac{5}{2}    &= 15 \\\\
(a + 3) \times 5               &= 15 \times 2 \\\\
(a + 3) \times 5               &= 30 \\\\
5a + 15                        &= 30 \\\\
5a                             &= 30 - 15 \\\\
5a                             &= 15 \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac{15}{5} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac{5 \times 3}{5}\\\\
a                              &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 3}{\cancel 5}\\\\
a                              &= 5 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$\text{BCA} \rightarrow $
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(((a + 3) \div 2 ) \times 5 )  &= 15 \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= \dfrac{15}{5} \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= \dfrac{5 \times 3}{5} \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= \dfrac{\cancel 5 \times 3}{\cancel 5} \\\\
(a + 3) \div 2                 &= 3 \\\\
(a + 3)                        &= 3 \times 2 \\\\
a + 3                          &= 6 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$\text{CBA} \rightarrow $

$
\begin{aligned}
(((a \div 2 ) + 3) \times 5 )   &= 15 \\\\

a \div 2                        &= 0 \\\\
a                               &= 0 \times 2 \\\\
a                               &= 0
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question question'>

A set of $5$ square based boxes are made from centimetre cubes 
and each box fits exactly inside each other, with the 
tops level. The dimensions of the largest box are $14 \times 14 \times 13$.
